Theorem 215.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: For convergence analysis pairs (A,B) in semiconductors theory, the coupling constant satisfies g ≤ 334 and the invariant I(A,B) = 423 mod 4.

Proof: By the convergence analysis principle applied to semiconductors theory, we decompose the problem into 334 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 423. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(4/n). QED.

Theorem 215.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Let the semiconductors structure S satisfy the conditions of structural decomposition framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 577. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 646 and converges at rate O(1/n^4).

Proof: Consider the structural decomposition operator L on the semiconductors space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 577 for all k. By the structural decomposition theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 646. QED.

Theorem 215.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: For any semiconductors module M satisfying the optimality condition hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 100. The projective resolution has length at most 490 with Betti numbers bounded by 3.

Proof: We apply the optimality condition principle to the semiconductors decomposition. By minimality, the element a^100 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 490-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.
